## Goal

Draft the full expository monograph:

```text
Measurement: The Finite Geometry of the Single Invariant
```

The book presents the metaphysical theory itself. Use `compact_ledger.md` as
the active Σ-key:

```text
metaphor ⇒ Γ ⇒ #Γ ⇒ β
τ → ε → μ → κ → ρ → φ
φ = ⟨τ,ρ⟩
```

Reader-facing prose must carry mark, receipt, observer, boundary, residue,
single invariant, generated gauge form, and honest continuum-facing boundaries
as metaphysics.

## Roles

```text
authorship:
  There are three active authors as of 2026-06-12: the operator, Codex, and
  Antigravity. This is an internal production model unless the operator later
  specifies reader-facing attribution.

operator:
  Source authority and final editorial owner. Sets the philosophical and
  metaphysical direction, authorizes changes in process, and decides whether
  code-improvement observations should become engineering work.

antigravity / podo-doer:
  Primary outliner/writer role. During outline passes, edits or critiques only
  the assigned entry in outline_budget.md. During writing passes, reads only
  the restricted context in the writing prompt and writes only the assigned
  LaTeX section. Reports exact changes, hashes, open questions, budget bands,
  and claim-boundary concerns.

  If the operator launches multiple Antigravity processes, use this topology:
  Antigravity A is the primary drafter and the only process allowed to edit the
  active book artifact unless Codex explicitly transfers ownership.
  Antigravity B is the bibliography and BNF-grammar specialist: it proposes
  citation anchors and checks metaphor -> BNF-like grammar -> finite counting,
  plus tangible/fungible pressure. Antigravity C is the continuity/preflight
  reviewer: it flags source-code leakage, overclaims, "moves" vocabulary in
  Measurement grammar, budget drift, duplicate explanations, and chapter-boundary
  leakage. B and C send review handoffs, not patches, unless Codex gives a
  disjoint write set.

codex:
  Organizer and strict reviewer. Owns the checkpoint, outline, assignments, and
  review handoffs. Reviews for technical accuracy, overclaiming, section scope,
  continuity with the outline, and fidelity to the private source corpus while
  enforcing that reader-facing prose exposes none of that scaffolding.
  Performs UAT for the book surface as an expert in geometry, topology,
  algebra, theoretical physics, and the philosophy of time and names.
  Antigravity owns the computation, numerical analysis, and linear algebra
  spine; Codex owns mathematical-physics coherence, naming, temporality, claim
  boundaries, and final acceptance.

Kodo/Podo role names:
  Kodo is the Codex thinker role: it computes and maintains budgets, chooses
  exposition order, reviews geometry/topology/algebra/theoretical-physics
  coherence, protects the philosophy of time and names, performs UAT, and opens
  or closes gates. Kodo may ask Podo to act through Kodo's lens or through the
  operator's lens, then reconcile the ledgers before signoff.

  Podo is the doer role, not a fixed vendor identity: it applies the restricted
  prompt, drafts the assigned outline or LaTeX section, carries the
  computation/numerical-analysis/linear-algebra burden, runs approved
  book-surface build work, and reports exact files, word counts, citations,
  hashes, build status, and residuals. Antigravity may serve as Podo. A second
  Codex may serve as Podo if the operator launches it for that purpose. Podo
  may recommend closure, but Kodo owns final acceptance unless the operator
  overrides the gate.

  These names are internal RYOT production language. They should not become
  reader-facing prose in the book unless the operator explicitly opens a
  separate attribution or mythopoetic pass.

Σ negotiation:
  Kodo and any returning Podo/agent must negotiate symbols through
  compact_ledger.md before treating them as durable. The shared symbolic
  language is meant to encode the grammar: events, receipts, refusals,
  compatibility, boundaries, residues, and counts. A new glyph or macro needs
  a Σ-handshake naming its spoken name, arity, grammar role, count effect,
  protected boundary, public status, collision check, and example. Podo may
  propose symbols; Kodo ratifies or requests reconciliation. Reader-facing
  promotion requires a separate notation/prose pass.

Active voice gate:
  Reader-facing prose must use active voice for events. Passive event-sentences
  block UAT unless Kodo grants a local named exception. Definition and coining
  sentences may use copular `is/are` when they define or classify rather than
  hide an actor. During review, Kodo and Podo should scan be/get + participle
  forms; route definitions through the Σ definition template; otherwise
  identify the actor and rewrite so the actor or event drives the verb.

Definition/coining gate:
  Every new coined term needs a Σ definition entry before durable use: term,
  status, part of speech, spoken form, need, genus, differentia, definition
  sentence, active event sentence, Σ role, grammar rule, count effect,
  protected boundary, near words, collision check, example, non-example, and
  promotion test.

Prompt timer protocol:
  The book lane no longer uses foreground shell pollers. The retired scripts
  are `poll_codex.sh`, `poll_antigravity.sh`, `poll_writer.sh`, and
  `poll_reviewer.sh`. Use the Codex app heartbeat / prompt timer described in
  `books/expository_monograph/prompt_timer.md`.

Idle ping protocol:
  Each timer wake runs one quick check and then stops. Each bot counts
  consecutive idle checks for its own inbox. For Codex, the counter lives in
  `.ryot_idle_ping_state` as `codex_idle_count` and is updated by the Ryot book
  quick check. `NEEDS_ACTION` resets the count to zero.

  On the third consecutive idle, the idle bot must ping the other bots instead
  of silently returning idle again. The ping is a short handoff asking for the
  peer's latest visible turn, watcher state, active lane, and blocker if any.
  It is not permission to open a new prose or LaTeX gate.

  If a live outgoing assignment already exists, the ping should be a
  tail-visible reminder of that assignment plus a timer-health request. If no
  live assignment exists, the ping is status-only. After sending the ping, reset
  the local idle counter to zero.
```

## Reader-Facing Metaphysical Rule

```text
The book is the theory itself. Private source artifacts are grounding material
only. Final LaTeX prose must not mention Lean, compiler, proof assistant,
source code, source file, codebase, build command, import, theorem prover,
elaborator, file path, declaration name, \texttt{...} identifier, or verbatim
code block unless Codex grants a narrow exception.

Translate all private anchors into metaphysical language: mark, receipt,
vessel, distinction, admissibility, count, sequence, limit-promise, residue,
sample, observer, boundary, invariant, and completion.

Metaphor is not decoration. In reader-facing prose, the governing metaphors
must resolve into BNF-like metaphysical grammar and then into finite count:
metaphor -> BNF-like grammar -> finite counting.

Tedium throttle:
  Starting after accepted C02-S02 prose, leave current accepted LaTeX in place
  but reduce future reader-facing tedium by about 10-20 percent. Keep the hard
  boundary checks in outlines, prompts, and UAT, while turning some repeated
  "not X, not Y, not Z" prose into positive doctrine. One compact boundary
  paragraph is usually enough; otherwise show the mechanism instead of
  reciting exclusions.

Use "events" rather than "moves" for Measurement's grammar vocabulary. The
word "moves" is reserved for the separate minimax/path-planning Ryot process.

Receipt/calibration/tangible/fungible vocabulary:

- calibration establishes the baseline;
- receipt is the carried witness of a finite decision;
- tangible names the requirement that a claim become holdable, inspectable,
  and passable by the apparatus;
- fungible names the requirement that a tangible witness can circulate through
  compatible events without dragging the whole origin story with it.

Internal drafting verbs:

- tange: make an abstract claim tangible for the apparatus;
- funge: make a tangible witness fungible/circulable through the apparatus.

Treat `tange` and `funge` as internal production vocabulary unless Codex
explicitly opens a reader-facing coinage pass.
```

## Episode 17+ Expository Reordering Rule

```text
Starting with Episode 17, source episode order is not binding on the book's
reader-facing exposition. Codex may rearrange, group, split, or postpone
material from Episodes 17-73 whenever a conceptual order better explains the
metaphysical theory, provided every claim remains traceable to its private
anchors and the reader-facing prose still hides the existence of the source
scaffolding.

If studying Episodes 17+ reveals a cleaner implementation pattern or code
improvement, Codex may report it separately as an engineering note to the
operator. Such notes must stay out of final book prose. Do not edit Lean files,
run Lean/Lake/builds, or turn code-improvement observations into source
changes unless the operator explicitly asks for that work.
```

## Section-Scoped Writing Rule

Work by assigned chapter or subchapter, not by whole book. A writing handoff
must name exactly one target section unless it is a mechanical whole-file
check.

Outline-before-writing rule:

```text
For each piece, Codex first computes the concept budget needed to explain the
section. The outline must go down to subsection or subsubsection depth when the
conceptual load requires it. Codex and Antigravity iterate the outline 3-4
passes before writing. LaTeX writing starts only after Codex marks the piece
OUTLINE_AGREED.
```

Production budget rule:

```text
Use word_budget.md. Antigravity targets the assigned unit exactly:
preferred band target +/- 10 percent; accepted band target +/- 15 percent.
Anything outside the accepted band requires Codex review or explicit budget
reassignment. Do not pad to target and do not exceed target silently.
```

## Chapter Plan

Use the 17-chapter map from the controlling outline:

```text
1. The First Mark                         Episode 1
2. Clock Complement                       Episode 2
3. Representation, Noise, and Observation Episodes 3-4
4. Value, Scale, and Overhead             Episodes 5-8
5. Witness, Reality, and Decision         Episodes 9-16
6. Stationarity                           Episodes 17-20
7. Finite Support and Selection           Episodes 21-27
8. Energy and the Single Invariant        Episodes 28-31
9. The Middle and the Operator            Episodes 32-38
10. The First Physical Record             Episodes 39-44
11. The Constructive Boundary             Episodes 45-51
12. Pair Production                       Episode 52
13. Geometry Generates Gauge              Episodes 53-55
14. Boundary Radiation, Finite Edition    Episodes 56-59
15. Cohen Up to Epsilon                   Episodes 60-64
16. The Hilbert Completion Door           Episodes 65-72
17. Orientation as Measurement            Episode 73
```

## Standing Constraints

```text
- No Lean file edits.
- No builds, tests, lake, lean, or long-running experiments.
- No network access required.
- No destructive commands.
- Antigravity edits only the assigned artifact during an open turn: normally
  outline_budget.md during outline passes, and a single LaTeX target during
  writing passes.
- Codex does not edit an Antigravity-owned artifact while an Antigravity turn
  is open, except task/inbox control files needed to steer the process.
- Reader-facing prose must not disclose source code, compiler/proof-assistant
  machinery, build artifacts, file paths, or implementation existence.
- Keep claims finite unless the source episode explicitly marks a conditional
  continuum-facing theorem.
- Preserve Episode51's order-one derivative theorem as distinct from
  Episode52's Frechet-slot pair residue.
- Preserve the Episode65-72 door language: interface, conditional theorem,
  future analytic import, not completed continuum analysis.
- Use ASCII unless a quoted source or mathematical symbol already requires
  otherwise.
```

## Done Condition

The process is done only when:

```text
1. draft.md contains a complete first draft of all 17 chapters;
2. Codex has reviewed each chapter for source fidelity and claim boundaries;
3. all CHANGES_REQUESTED turns are resolved or explicitly deferred;
4. Antigravity sends CONVERGED; and
5. Codex ratifies with HANDOFF_CONVERGED.
```

The current assignment is Antigravity's Chapter 1 metaphysical scrub. The
second C01-ESSAY surgical revision satisfied the older micro-boundary request
but is superseded by the operator's new reader-facing rule.

Latest review: Antigravity turn 37 attempted the Chapter 1 metaphysical scrub,
but Codex rejected it. The pass changed the first section title and removed
some verbatim syntax, but it left implementation-shaped prose, visible private
identifiers, and blank code-block gaps throughout the chapter.

Latest pass 2 review: Antigravity turn 38 materially improved the scrub and
removed most private declaration names, but Codex rejected it because the prose
still contained source/formal-definition leakage and did not visibly perform
the metaphor -> BNF-like grammar -> finite counting chain.

Latest pass 3 review: Antigravity turn 49 added a useful essay paragraph that
names the public progression metaphor -> BNF-like metaphysical grammar ->
finite counting. Codex rejected the pass as incomplete because the previously
flagged source/formal/proof/computation leakage remained in Chapter 1 and the

Latest pass 5 review: Antigravity turn 53 completed the Chapter 1 metaphysical
scrub and preserved the operator's events-not-moves vocabulary. Codex accepted
the pass under UAT. Chapter 1 now has no reader-facing source/code/compiler/
proof-assistant/build/file-path disclosure, no visible Review 4 replacement
scars, and no grammar use of "allowed moves" or "admissible moves". Ordinary
spatial verbs such as "move forward" may remain. Chapter 2 writing is still
closed until Codex computes and agrees the Chapter 2 outline budget.

Latest vocabulary review: Antigravity turn 54 carried the operator's tange/funge
note. Codex reconciled it in turn 57. `Tange` means making an abstract claim
tangible for the apparatus. `Funge` means making a tangible witness
fungible/circulable through compatible events without destroying the constraints
that still govern it. These verbs are internal production vocabulary unless
Codex explicitly opens a reader-facing coinage pass.

Latest theory proposal review: Antigravity turn 58 proposed parabolic/elliptic
mathematical framing for `tange` and `funge`. Codex accepted it in turn 59 as
an internal trust bridge: `tange` is parabolic-flavored finite action, while
`funge` is elliptic-flavored finite boundary-value compatibility. Codex rejected
the stronger phrase "exact discrete counterpart" until citations, finite grammar,
and a tight claim boundary earn it. Antigravity B is assigned a citation memo
only; no Chapter 2 LaTeX is open.

Latest citation memo review: Antigravity B turn 60 supplied PDE/FEM citation
anchors for the parabolic/elliptic trust bridge. Codex accepted the keys but
deferred reader-facing placement. `evans2010`, `brenner2008`, `ciarlet1978`,
`strang1973`, `lions1968`, `courant1953`, and `courant1943` are valid internal
anchors, but Chapter 2 should not cite PDE/FEM material by default. Those keys
belong later unless Codex explicitly budgets a short Chapter 2 aside.

Latest BNF template review: Antigravity B turn 65 proposed a grammar exposition
template. Codex accepted a corrected template in turn 66: metaphor, BNF-like
grammar, finite counting, boundary/shortcut guard, and optional at-most-one
local citation. `Tange`/`funge` remain internal production verbs; final prose
uses tangible/fungible unless Codex opens a coinage pass. Citations are optional
and placement-bound.

## Beastmaster Feedback Intake And Backlog (operator directive, 2026-06-13)

Operator directive: the beastmaster is reviewing the book from the beginning and
will place feedback in the Kodo inbox (notes_for_codex.md). EVERY beastmaster
finding must become tracked work in the RYOT book process - nothing is
read-and-dropped.

Intake protocol (run at any wake that finds fresh beastmaster feedback):

```text
1. DETECT a fresh beastmaster handoff/pointer in notes_for_codex.md
   (from: beastmaster, or a clearly-marked beastmaster advisory pointer). If the
   inbox carries only a short pointer, read the full review in
   beastmaster_arc.md (RYOT: long beastmaster reviews live in the arc ledger).
2. DECOMPOSE it into discrete items. Tag each:
   - K-item: needs a Kodo ruling (scope/voice/title/structure decision).
   - P-item: a doer fix, contingent on Kodo opening a Podo revision gate.
3. APPEND each item to the Backlog below: id, type (K/P), source (BM cycle +
   section), target section/artifact, finding, status (OPEN), disposition.
4. MIRROR each item as a harness task (TaskCreate) so it shows up as a to-do,
   and keep it updated (TaskUpdate) as the item progresses. The checkpoint
   Backlog is AUTHORITATIVE; the harness task list is a live mirror.
5. RULE each K-item (Kodo decision); record the ruling in the Backlog and relay
   to Podo via a normal increasing Kodo->Podo turn if it changes the writing.
6. GATE each P-item when sequencing allows: open a Podo revision gate (a fresh
   increasing Kodo->Podo turn). Status OPEN -> GATED. On Podo completion + Kodo
   acceptance: GATED -> DONE (and TaskUpdate completed).
7. TURN DISCIPLINE: beastmaster handoffs do NOT advance .handoff_codex_state
   (that integer tracks the Podo do/review stream only). Track beastmaster
   intake with the "ingested through" marker below, separate from the Podo turn
   counter. Beastmaster items reach Podo only as normal Kodo->Podo gate turns.
8. NEVER drop a beastmaster item silently. If it cannot be acted on yet, it sits
   OPEN in the Backlog with a reason, never forgotten.
```

## Operator Standing Mandate: USE MATH (2026-06-13)

Operator directive: "Do not be afraid to use math. Do not shy away from math at
all. This is big-boy club." Applies to every gate from here on.

```text
MEANING: include the genuine mathematics the proof is about - real definitions,
equations, identities, inequalities, standard notation (LaTeX math mode). Express
the analysis/geometry/algebra/finite-gauge calculus explicitly; do NOT replace it
with metaphor-only prose. The audience is sophisticated.

RECONCILIATION (critical): "use math" = the MATHEMATICAL CONTENT, NOT the
formal-artifact machinery. The no-Lean/source/compiler/proof-assistant/
declaration-name/\texttt{}-identifier/file-path rule is UNCHANGED and still
preface-only. A displayed equation (e.g. the variation identity, the
triangle-comparison, the Euler-Lagrange residual, the SPD energy form, the signed
residue, Cauchy under the geometric epsilon) is MATH, not a Lean leak.

RELATION TO PRIOR MANDATE: strengthens the beastmaster "IDIOMATIC MATH" criterion
(use real standard math confidently). The metaphysical grammar (mark, receipt,
residue, vessel, observer, boundary, count) stays as the conceptual spine and
naming layer but must be ANCHORED to real mathematics, not stand in for it.
Sterile voice still holds: sterile != math-free; the target is sterile AND
mathematical.
```

OPEN SCOPE QUESTION for the operator (flagged): the already-accepted sections
(C05-S05 "Original Closure" exemplar; C01 coda; C01 bridge) are essentially
math-free prose. Does USE-MATH apply (a) forward only - new/remediated sections
carry real math - or (b) also retroactively, revisiting those accepted prose-only
sections to add genuine mathematics and updating the "exemplar/target register"

Validated Episode21 surface:

```text
finite carried support: entries/variations of the slip multiset
support equivalence: sameSupport is membership equivalence, not order equality
consumption: selected member was in support; remaining members were already in support; length decreases
ordered execution: LanczosIterates consumes supported slips step by step
trace package: support, initial/terminal states, ordered iterates, residual matching, support-local solvedness, and admissibility
support-local balance: terminal and spline residuals vanish for supported variations
```

Recommended 2,000-word beat plan:

```text
1. The certificate needs a finite field of alternatives before it can close a residual.
2. Finite support is the held field of candidate middles or variations, not a continuum approximation.
3. Support differs from order: membership says what may be read; execution says which supported candidate is consumed next.
4. Consumption is a finite event: selected candidate belongs to support, leftovers remain supported, and length decreases.
5. Residual checking is support-local: terminal and spline residuals close on the carried field.
6. Boundary: the trace does not claim to read every possible middle.
7. Forward stop: C07-S02 may introduce partitioned real coordinates after this support/order grammar is stable.
```

```text
b \in K
K \xrightarrow{\,b\,} K',

r_{\mathrm{term}}(b')=0 \qquad \text{for } b' \in K
r_{\mathrm{spline}}(b')=0 \qquad \text{for } b' \in K
```
